},"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6bc63180-9263-4b3d-b5b5-34a4f46e2eb7","uuid":"99a15c3c-6ad1-4a7a-a8ce-27a0b2a00b46","timestamp":"2026-08-12T07:53:12.155Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/codex/repos/ns006-1398-planeval/packages/telemetry/src/adapters/otel/otel-deno.ts","content":"import { metrics, propagation, trace } from '@opentelemetry/api';\nimport type {\n  Attributes,\n  Context,\n  Link,\n  Meter,\n  MeterPort,\n  ObservableCallback,\n  PropagationExtractCarrier,\n  PropagationInjectCarrier,\n  PropagatorPort,\n  SpanContext,\n  SpanLinkPort,\n  TelemetryProviderDescriptor,\n  TracerProviderPort,\n} from '../../ports/mod.ts';\n\n/**\n * Capability descriptor for the default Deno-native OpenTelemetry provider.\n *\n * Deno's runtime auto-registers a global tracer provider when `OTEL_DENO=true`,\n * so this adapter performs no explicit registration. The Deno-native provider\n * does not yet preserve per-link attributes, which the descriptor advertises.\n */\nexport const otelDenoDescriptor: TelemetryProviderDescriptor = {\n  id: 'otel-deno',\n  description: \"Deno runtime's auto-registered global OpenTelemetry provider.\",\n  supportsLinkAttributes: false,\n};\n\n/**\n * Provider adapter that binds to Deno's auto-registered global OpenTelemetry\n * provider.\n *\n * This is the default provider for NetScript telemetry. Because the Deno\n * runtime registers the global provider itself when `OTEL_DENO=true`,\n * {@linkcode OtelDenoTracerProvider.register} is a documented no-op; user code\n * must not call `setGlobalTracerProvider` on top of it.\n */\nexport class OtelDenoTracerProvider implements TracerProviderPort {\n  /** Capability descriptor advertising attribute-less links. */\n  readonly descriptor: TelemetryProviderDescriptor = otelDenoDescriptor;\n\n  /**\n   * No-op: the Deno runtime auto-registers the global provider when\n   * `OTEL_DENO=true`. Present to satisfy {@linkcode TracerProviderPort}.\n   */\n  register(): void {\n    // Intentionally empty; Deno owns global provider registration.\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Report whether a non-noop tracer provider is registered in this process.\n   *\n   * Returns `false` when telemetry is disabled (Deno leaves the built-in\n   * no-op provider in place) and `true` once the runtime has installed its\n   * OTLP-backed provider.\n   */\n  isActive(): boolean {\n    const provider = trace.getTracerProvider();\n    return provider?.constructor?.name !== 'NoopTracerProvider';\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Propagator adapter delegating to the global OpenTelemetry propagator that the\n * Deno runtime registers.\n *\n * Inject/extract flow through `@opentelemetry/api`'s global propagation API, so\n * the active W3C Trace Context propagator (installed by the runtime) does the\n * work; when telemetry is off the global propagator is a documented no-op.\n */\nexport class OtelDenoPropagator implements PropagatorPort {\n  /** Inject the current trace context into `carrier` as W3C headers. */\n  inject(context: Context, carrier: PropagationInjectCarrier): void {\n    propagation.inject(context, carrier);\n  }\n\n  /** Extract a trace context from `carrier`, falling back to `context`. */\n  extract(context: Context, carrier: PropagationExtractCarrier): Context {\n    return propagation.extract(context, carrier);\n  }\n\n  /** Carrier keys the global propagator reads and writes. */\n  fields(): string[] {\n    return propagation.fields();\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Meter adapter delegating to the global OpenTelemetry meter that the Deno\n * runtime registers.\n *\n * {@linkcode OtelDenoMeter.forceFlush} is a no-op: the Deno runtime owns the\n * metric export pipeline, so there is nothing for the adapter to drain.\n */\nexport class OtelDenoMeter implements MeterPort {\n  /** Obtain a {@linkcode Meter} from the global meter provider. */\n  getMeter(name: string, version?: string): Meter {\n    const meter = metrics.getMeter(name, version);\n    return {\n      createCounter: (counterName, options) => {\n        const counter = meter.createCounter(counterName, options);\n        return {\n          add: (value: number, attributes?: Attributes) => counter.add(value, attributes),\n        };\n      },\n      createHistogram: (histogramName, options) => {\n        const histogram = meter.createHistogram(histogramName, options);\n        return {\n          record: (value: number, attributes?: Attributes) => histogram.record(value, attributes),\n        };\n      },\n      createObservableGauge: (gaugeName, options) => {\n        const gauge = meter.createObservableGauge(gaugeName, options);\n        return {\n          addCallback: (callback: ObservableCallback) => gauge.addCallback(callback),\n          removeCallback: (callback: ObservableCallback) => gauge.removeCallback(callback),\n        };\n      },","numLines":120,"startLine":0,"totalLines":179}}}

appears.\n *\n * Startup job snapshots and other stream traffic are retained as observed identities but never\n * contribute links to the returned Flow-B consumer receipt.\n */\nexport async function selectFlowBStreamChange(\n  identity: FlowBProducerIdentity,\n  initialState: StreamSseReplayStateV1,\n  options: FlowBSelectionOptions,\n): Promise<FlowBSelectionReceipt> {\n  if (!Number.isInteger(options.maxBatches) || options.maxBatches < 1) {\n    throw new Error('Flow-B selection maxBatches must be a positive integer');\n  }\n  if (!Number.isFinite(options.timeoutMs) || {"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"gen-1786521198-rmpa9A0ZbM8PCNoANDut","type":"message","role":"assistant","container":null,"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Good — TC-14 confirms that the gate checks **trace id only** (it extracts the trace id from the traceparent and compares it). The selector returns the **first** match (line 99 returns immediately on the first match). \n\nNow this is critical: **the selector returns on the first match**. So if `create()` runs first (it does, at `job-dispatcher.ts:74-86` before the span exists), and the published record's `correlationId` is set, **this record will be matched first** and TC-14 will be evaluated against the `create()` record's trace id — not the `complete()` record's. \n\nThe plan claims D3 ensures *every* record's publish span shares the trace id with `job.execute`. Let me confirm whether `getParentContextFromHeaders` produces a context that becomes the trace id.\n\nLooking at `extractContext` (w3c.ts:198-232), when given the **stored** traceparent (from the execution record), it builds a remote span context, sets that as the active span, and returns. When OTel's `tracer.startSpan(name, options)` is called without an explicit parent context, it uses `context.active()`. Since `withSpan` sets the active span context via `context.with(trace.setSpan(parentContext, span), ...)`, the new `traceJobExecution` span inherits the trace id from the stored traceparent.\n\nSo `job.execute`'s trace id = the trace id stored in the dispatch's `traceparent` (which is now also stored in the execution record at `job-dispatcher.ts:83-84`). \n\nNow for D3 to work, the **publish span** must be started while the execution's stored traceparent is the active context. Since `startPublish` is called synchronously from the mutation hook (`executionState.create/save → setMutationHook(...) → publish.upsert`), if the mutation hook wraps the call with `context.with(extracted, () => producer.upsert(...))`, the trace id of the publish span will match the stored trace id.\n\nBut here's a critical issue: the **producer is a module-level singleton** (`producer.ts:28-44`), constructed once. The publish operation starts the span and queues asynchronously. If the publish call is wrapped with `context.with(...)`, the span starts inside the context — but then `enqueue` schedules delivery for later, *outside* the context. That doesn't matter for the span header (which is captured at startSpan time), so the traceparent header is correct.\n\nNow let me check the gated tests and the deferral:","citations":[]}],"model":"minimax/minimax-m3","stop_reason":null,"stop_details":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"output_tokens":0,"output_tokens_details":null,"cache_creation_input_tokens":null,"cache_read_input_tokens":null,"cache_creation":null,"inference_geo":null,"server_tool_use":null,"service_tier":null,"speed":"standard"},"provider":"Novita","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6bc63180-9263-4b3d-b5b5-34a4f46e2eb7","uuid":"3358cda5-1762-40cc-82d3-8b5a96dec29f","timestamp":"2026-08-12T07:53:27.794Z"}

}\n});\n\n// #954: the suite used to start the whole AppHost without ever waiting on the generated app\n// or requesting one of its routes, so \"Aspire says Healthy while every request returns resolveSuite(SCAFFOLD.RUNTIME_SQLITE);\n    assertEquals(sqlite.defaultOptions.database, DATABASE.SQLITE);\n    assertEquals(sqlite.defaultOptions.cache, false);\n assertEquals(Deno.env.get(environmentVariable), operatorCacheMode);\n  } finally {\n    if (previous === undefined) Deno.env.delete(environmentVariable);\n    else Deno.env.set(environmentVariable,   const verify = ids.indexOf(GATE.BEHAVIOR_SERVICE_ENV);\n    const patchesGeneratedHelpers = [\n      ids.indexOf(GATE.RUNTIME_FLOW_B_FIXTURE),\n      ids.indexOf(GATE.RUNTIME_READINESS_FIXTURE),\n    ];\n\n    // The fixture edits appsettings.json and regenerates; a declaration written\n    // after the AppHost is already up would prove nothing about the running\n    // resource, and the after a fixture\n    // that hand-patched one — that would silently erase the patch.\n    for (const patched of patchesGeneratedHelpers) {\n      assertEquals(\n        fixture < patched,\n '../domain/constants.ts';\n1\t\n2\tinterface EnvReadState {\n3\t  readonly deniedKeys: string[];\n4\t}\n5\t\n6\tfunction createEnvReadState(): EnvReadState {\n7\t  return { deniedKeys: [] };\n8\t}\n9\t\n10\tfunction isPermissionDenied(error: unknown): boolean {\n11\t  return error instanceof Error &&\n12\t    (error.name === 'PermissionDenied' || error.constructor.name === 'PermissionDenied');\n13\t}\n14\t\n15\tfunction readDenoEnv(key: string, state: EnvReadState): string | undefined {\n16\t  try {\n17\t    return Deno.env.get(key);\n18\t  } catch (error) {\n19\t    if (isPermissionDenied(error)) {\n20\t      state.deniedKeys.push(key);\n21\t      return undefined;\n22\t    }\n23\t    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n24\t    throw new Error(`Could not read environment variable \"${key}\": ${message}`);\n25\t  }\n26\t}\n27\t\n28\tfunction getServerServiceEndpoint(\n29\t  serviceName: string,\n30\t  state: EnvReadState,\n31\t  protocol: 'http' | 'https' = 'http',\n32\t  index = 0,\n33\t): string | undefined {\n34\t  if (typeof Deno === 'undefined') {\n35\t    return undefined;\n36\t  }\n37\t\n38\t  return readDenoEnv(`services__${serviceName}__${protocol}__${index}`, state);\n39\t}\n40\t\n41\t/**\n42\t * Read a service URL from the browser environment.\n43\t *\n44\t * Mirrors the lookup performed by `@netscript/sdk/discovery` so that browser\n45\t * consumers (Fresh islands, the Vite-built playground) can resolve the streams\n46\t * URL without taking a framework-level dependency on the SDK.\n47\t *\n48\t * Aspire's `WithConfiguredViteHttpReferences` injects two env vars per\n49\t * service reference, both surfaced by Vite as `import.meta.env.VITE_*`:\n50\t *   1. `VITE_services__{name}__{protocol}__{index}` — isomorphic full format.\n51\t *   2. `VITE_{NORMALISED}_URL` — convenience shorthand.\n52\t */\n53\tfunction getBrowserServiceEndpoint(\n54\t  serviceName: string,\n55\t  protocol: 'http' | 'https' = 'http',\n56\t  index = 0,\n57\t): string | undefined {\n58\t  try {\n59\t    const env = readImportMetaEnvironment(import.meta);\n60\t    if (!env) return undefined;\n61\t\n62\t    const fullKey = `VITE_services__${serviceName}__${protocol}__${index}`;\n63\t    const fullUrl = env[fullKey];\n64\t    if (fullUrl) return fullUrl;\n65\t\n66\t    const shortKey = `VITE_${serviceName.toUpperCase().replace(/-/g, '_')}_URL`;\n67\t    return env[shortKey];\n68\t  } catch {\n69\t    return undefined;\n70\t  }\n71\t}\n72\t\n73\tfunction readImportMetaEnvironment(\n74\t  meta: ImportMeta,\n75\t): Readonly<Record<string, string | undefined>> | undefined {\n76\t  if (!('env' in meta) || !isEnvironmentRecord(meta.env)) return undefined;\n77\t  return meta.env;\n78\t}\n79\t\n80\tfunction isEnvironmentRecord(\n81\t  value: unknown,\n82\t): value is Readonly<Record<string, string | undefined>> {\n83\t  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null &&\n84\t    Object.values(value).every((entry: unknown) =>\n85\t      entry === undefined || typeof entry === 'string'\n86\t    );\n87\t}\n88\t\n89\t/**\n90\t * Resolve the base URL of the durable streams server.\n91\t *\n92\t * Works in both server (Deno) and browser contexts. endpoints.at(-1)?.[1];\nif (!endpoint) throw new Error('Aspire OTLP/HTTP endpoint was not found');\n\nconst provider = createTelemetryProvider({\n  providerId: 'otel-sdk',\n  options: { endpoint, serviceName: 'flow-b-stream-consumer' },\n  loadSdk: createFlowBSdkLoader(endpoint),\n});\nawait provider.register();\n\ntry {\n  const flowBProducer = await readJobExecuteIdentity(metadata.dashboardUrl);\n  const streamPort = Number(Deno.args[1]);\n  if (!Number.isInteger(streamPort)) throw new Error('streams port argument is required');\n  const streamUrl = `http://127.0.0.1:${streamPort}/v1/stream/netscript/workers/executions`;\n  let response = await fetch(\n    `${streamUrl}?offset=-1`,\n    {\n      headers: { accept: 'application/json' },\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(15_000),\n    },\n  );\n  if (response.status === 404) {\n    const traceId = randomHex(16);\n    const spanId = randomHex(8);\n    const createResponse = await fetch(streamUrl, {\n      method: 'PUT',\n      headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },\n      body: JSON.stringify({\n        type: 'execution',\n        key: 'flow-b-e2e',\n        value: { correlationId: 'flow-b-e2e' },\n        headers: {\n          operation: 'upsert',\n          traceparent: `00-${traceId}-${spanId}-01`,\n        },\n      }),\n    });\n    if (!createResponse.ok && createResponse.status !== 409) {\n      throw new Error(`workers stream create failed: HTTP ${createResponse.status}`);\n    }\n    response = await fetch(`${streamUrl}?offset=-1`, {\n      headers: { accept: 'application/json' },\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(15_000),\n    });\n  }\n  if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`workers stream read failed: HTTP ${response.status}`);\n  await response.body?.cancel();\n\n  const selected = await selectFlowBStreamChange(\n    flowBProducer,\n    createStreamSseReplayStateV1(),\n    {\n      maxBatches: FLOW_B_SELECTION_MAX_BATCHES,\n      timeoutMs: FLOW_B_SELECTION_TIMEOUT_MS,\n      retryDelayMs: FLOW_B_SELECTION_RETRY_DELAY_MS,\n      readBatch: (state, timeoutMs) => consumeNamedStreamEvents(streamUrl, state, timeoutMs),\n    },\n  );\n  const selectedCorrelationId = streamChangeCorrelationId(selected.change);\n  if (selectedCorrelationId !== flowBProducer.correlationId) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `Flow-B selected correlation changed unexpectedly; expected=${flowBProducer.correlationId} actual=${\n        selectedCorrelationId ?? '<missing>'\n      }`,\n    );\n  }\n  const messages = [toFanInMessage(selected.change)];\n  if (!selected.state.lastCommittedOffset) {\n    throw new Error('named SSE control did not commit a replay offset');\n  }\n  const replay = await consumeNamedStreamEvents(streamUrl, selected.state);\n  if (!replay.outcomes.some((event) => event.event === 'heartbeat')) {\n    throw new Error('offset reconnect did not receive an up-to-date control heartbeat');\n  }\n  const malformed = parseStreamSseEventV1({\n    eventName: 'control',\n    data: '{\"streamNextOffset\":7}',\n    lastCommittedOffset: selected.state.lastCommittedOffset,\n  });\n  if (malformed.ok || malformed.error.retryable) {\n    throw new Error('malformed control did not produce a non-retryable v1 error');\n  }\n  if (malformed.error.lastCommittedOffset !== selected.state.lastCommittedOffset) {\n    throw new Error('malformed control changed the last committed offset');\n  }\n  const documentedReceipt = await runDocumentedStreamExample(new URL(streamUrl).origin);\n  const span = createStreamsInstrumentation().startSubscribeSpan({\n    streamPath: '/workers/executions',\n    collection: 'execution',\n    operation: 'fan-in',\n    messages,\n  });\n  span.setAttribute('netscript.correlation.id', selectedCorrelationId);\n  span.setAttribute('netscript.stream.outcome', 'success');\n  span.setStatus({ code: SpanStatusCode.OK });\n  span.end();\n  await provider.forceFlush?.();\n  console.info(\n    `Flow-B named SSE consumer selected correlation ${selectedCorrelationId} after ${selected.batchesRead} batch(es), linked ${messages.length} message(s), committed ${selected.state.lastCommittedOffset}, reconnected to heartbeat, rejected malformed control, and ran the unchanged documented example over ${documentedReceipt} materialized record(s)`,\n  );\n} finally {\n  await provider.shutdown?.();\n}\n\nasync function readJobExecuteIdentity(dashboardUrl: unknown): Promise<FlowBProducerIdentity> {\n  if (typeof dashboardUrl !== 'string') {\n    throw new Error('Aspire start metadata did not contain dashboardUrl');\n  }\n  const tracesUrl = new URL('/api/telemetry/traces', dashboardUrl);\n  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= 20; attempt++) {\n    const response = await fetch(tracesUrl);\n    if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`Dashboard traces read failed: HTTP ${response.status}`);\n    const identity = findJobExecuteIdentity(await response.json());\n    if (identity) return identity;\n    if (attempt < 20) await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 500));\n  }\n  throw new Error('Flow-B job.execute telemetry did not expose correlation and trace identities');\n}\n\nfunction findJobExecuteIdentity(value: unknown): FlowBProducerIdentity | undefined {\n  if (Array.isArray(value)) {\n    for (const item of value) {\n      const found = findJobExecuteIdentity(item);\n      if (found) return found;\n    }\n    return undefined;\n  }\n  if (!isRecord(value)) return undefined;\n  if (value.name === 'job.execute' && Array.isArray(value.attributes)) {\n    const attributes = value.attributes;\n    const jobId = attributeString(attributes, ['netscript.job.id', 'job.id']);\n    const correlationId = attributeString(attributes, ['netscript.correlation.id']);\n    if (jobId === 'flow-b-callback' && correlationId && typeof value.traceId === 'string') {\n      return { correlationId, traceId: value.traceId };\n    }\n  }\n  for (const child of Object.values(value)) {\n    const found = findJobExecuteIdentity(child);\n    if (found) return found;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\nfunction attributeString(\n  attributes: readonly unknown[],\n  keys: readonly string[],\n): string | undefined {\n  for (const attribute of attributes) {\n    if (\n      !isRecord(attribute) || !keys.includes(String(attribute.key)) ||\n      !isRecord(attribute.value)\n    ) continue;\n    if (typeof attribute.value.stringValue === 'string') return attribute.value.stringValue;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\ninterface NamedStreamReceipt {\n  readonly changes: readonly StreamSseChangeV1[];\n  readonly outcomes: readonly StreamSseConsumerEventV1[];\n  readonly state: StreamSseReplayStateV1;\n}\n\nfunction consumeNamedStreamEvents(\n  streamUrl: string,\n  initialState: StreamSseReplayStateV1,\n  timeoutMs = 15_000,\n): Promise<NamedStreamReceipt> {\n  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n    const url = new URL(streamUrl);\n    url.searchParams.set('offset', initialState.lastCommittedOffset ?? '-1');\n    url.searchParams.set('live', 'sse');\n    const source = new EventSource(url);\n    const changes: StreamSseChangeV1[] = [];\n    const outcomes: StreamSseConsumerEventV1[] = [];\n    let settled = false;\n    const timeout = setTimeout(\n      () => finish(new Error(`named SSE control timed out after ${Math.ceil(timeoutMs)}ms`)),\n      timeoutMs,\n    );\n    const binding = bindStreamEventSourceV1({\n      source,\n      initialState,\n      onEvent(event) {\n        outcomes.push(event);\n        if (event.event === 'data') changes.push(...event.payload);\n        if (event.event === 'error') {\n          finish(new Error(`${event.payload.code}: ${event.payload.message}`));\n          return;\n        }\n        if (event.event === 'control' || event.event === 'heartbeat') {\n          finish();\n        }\n      },\n    });\n\n    function finish(error?: Error): void {\n      if (settled) return;\n      settled = true;\n      clearTimeout(timeout);\n      queueMicrotask(() => {\n        const state = binding.snapshot();\n        binding.dispose();\n        if (error) reject(error);\n        else resolve({ changes, outcomes, state });\n      });\n    }\n  });\n}\n\nfunction toFanInMessage(change: StreamSseChangeV1): {\n  traceparent: string;\n  tracestate?: string;\n  streamPath: string;\n  collection?: string;\n  operation?: string;\n  messageId?: string;\n  correlationId?: string;\n} {\n  return {\n    traceparent: change.headers.traceparent,\n    tracestate: change.headers.tracestate,\n    streamPath: '/workers/executions',\n    collection: change.type,\n    operation: change.headers.operation,\n    messageId: change.key,\n    correlationId: streamChangeCorrelationId(change),\n  };\n}\n\nfunction isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {\n  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);\n}\n\nfunction randomHex(byteLength: number): string {\n  return [...crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(byteLength))]\n    .map((value) => value.toString(16).padStart(2, '0'))\n    .join('');\n}\n\nfunction createFlowBSdkLoader(endpoint: string): SdkLoader {\n  return () => {\n    const exporter = createOtlpJsonSpanExporter(endpoint);\n    const processor = new SimpleSpanProcessor(exporter);\n    const tracerProvider = new BasicTracerProvider({ spanProcessors: [processor] });\n    return Promise.resolve({\n      tracerProvider: {\n        register: () => {\n          trace.setGlobalTracerProvider(tracerProvider);\n        },\n        forceFlush: () => tracerProvider.forceFlush(),\n        shutdown: () => tracerProvider.shutdown(),\n      },\n      meterProvider: {\n        forceFlush: () => Promise.resolve(),\n        shutdown: () => Promise.resolve(),\n      },\n    });\n  };","numLines":300,"startLine":0,"totalLines":363}}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"gen-1786521270-KKBOIFq0jEDTgr354Ge4","type":"message","role":"assistant","container":null,"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now let me look at how the OTel publish span gets a parent context. Looking back at `createStreamsInstrumentation.startPublish` (instrumentation.ts:160-170), the `startSpan` call on `StreamsTracerPort` does NOT pass any parent context. So how does D3 make the publish span inherit the trace id?\n\nFor D3 to work, the implementation needs to wrap the `producer.upsert(...)` call with `context.with(extracted, () => producer.upsert(...))`. But the producer is a singleton (constructed at module load), and the upsert is fire-and-forget. Let me check whether there's an alternative — perhaps the OTel `@opentelemetry/api` `context` is updated via `propagation.extract` so that spans created within its scope use that context as parent.\n\nLooking at the OTel API: `tracer.startSpan(name, options, ctx)` — if no explicit context is given, the SDK uses `context.active()`. So if `producer.upsert` is called inside `context.with(extractedContext, () => producer.upsert(...))`, the active context during `startSpan` will be `extractedContext`, and the new span will use it as parent (i.e., share its trace id).\n\nThe plan says D3 is implemented in the hook. Let me look at where the plan says the hook should be wrapped:","citations":[]}],"model":"minimax/minimax-m3","stop_reason":null,"stop_details":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"output_tokens":0,"output_tokens_details":null,"cache_creation_input_tokens":null,"cache_read_input_tokens":null,"cache_creation":null,"inference_geo":null,"server_tool_use":null,"service_tier":null,"speed":"standard"},"provider":"Novita","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6bc63180-9263-4b3d-b5b5-34a4f46e2eb7","uuid":"e9498904-9349-4b82-a4e7-7bdf61ea3243","timestamp":"2026-08-12T07:54:36.508Z"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"gen-1786521291-B0rI6Ij9sHD2YOo8nNM1","type":"message","role":"assistant","container":null,"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now this is critical: `StreamsInstrumentation.startPublish` (line 160-170 of instrumentation.ts) calls `this.#tracer.startSpan(name, options)`. The `StreamsTracerPort` interface (line 92-102) does NOT take a parent context — only `{ kind, attributes, links }`. So when D3 wraps the `producer.upsert` call with `context.with(extracted, ...)`, the `startSpan` call must rely on `context.active()` being the extracted context.\n\nLooking at how `createSpan` in `application/span.ts:13-26` does it: it uses `tracer.startSpan(name, spanOptions, parentContext)` — passing `parentContext` as the third arg (the `Context` arg). But `StreamsInstrumentation.startPublish` does not do that. It uses the `StreamsTracerPort` interface which **doesn't accept parent context**.\n\nSo how does the publish span inherit the parent context? The only way is via `context.active()`. If the caller wraps with `context.with(extracted, () => producer.upsert(...))`, then `context.active()` during the synchronous `startSpan` call will be `extracted`, and the new span will inherit the trace id.\n\nLet me verify this assumption by looking at the OTel SDK semantics. Looking at `span.ts` line 24-25:\n```ts\nconst parentContext = options.parentContext ?? context.active();\nreturn tracer.startSpan(name, spanOptions, parentContext);\n```\n\nWhen `parentContext` is undefined, the OTel SDK uses `context.active()` as parent. If the caller wraps with `context.with(...)`, `context.active()` returns the extracted context inside that block.\n\nSo D3 requires wrapping `producer.upsert` in `context.with(extractedContext, () => producer.upsert(...))`. **This is not currently done.** The hook calls `producer.upsert(...)` directly. D3 requires modifying the hook. This is a real implementation, not just \"ambient context works\". The plan's argument hinges on this wrapping.\n\nLet me now check whether `tracedContext?.parentContext ?? getParentContextFromHeaders(traceHeaders)` (line 43) is the correct path. Note that in `tracedContext?.parentContext`, the context is already provided by the caller — but in this code path (`processWorkerJob`), `tracedContext` is undefined when called from the queue. So it falls back to `getParentContextFromHeaders(traceHeaders)`. The traceHeaders come from the queue message, which contains the dispatch traceparent.\n\nThis means: the **stored** traceparent on the execution record IS the dispatch traceparent. And the `job.execute` span IS a child of that traceparent (because `traceJobExecution` calls `withSpan(tracer, ..., { parentContext })`).\n\nSo the trace id chain is: dispatch's stored traceparent → `job.execute` span → (currently) ambient context for `create()` → publish span (for `create()`). \n\nD3 needs to wrap the publish calls in `context.with(extractedFromStoredTraceparent, () => producer.upsert(...))`. This is a real, mechanical fix. Now let me verify that the implementation will actually make this work in production. But wait — `producer.upsert` is **fire-and-forget**, and the publish span starts **synchronously** during `upsert` (the publish header is captured at startSpan time, but actual delivery is queued). The context wrapping only needs to cover the synchronous `upsert` invocation.\n\nBut there's a subtlety: `producer.upsert` is on a singleton. The mutation hook is called from `setMutationHook` context, which is called from `executionState.create/save`. The `setMutationHook` callback is called from `KvExecutionState.#save` (line 304-307 of execution-state.ts), which runs as part of `await this.#kv.set(...)`. The `await` yields back to the event loop. So the **active context at the time the hook fires** could be different from the context at `create()` time.\n\nThis is a real concern. Let me re-check the chain:\n\n1. `executionState.create(...)` at `job-dispatcher.ts:73-86` runs **before** `traceJobExecution`. The stored `traceparent` is the **dispatch** traceparent (from queue message).\n2. `executionState.create` calls `validateExecution(...)` then `await this.#save(record, 'created')` (line 176-177).\n3. `#save` does `await this.#kv.set(...)` then `this.#onMutation?.({ type, execution: record })` (line 304-307).\n4. The hook calls `producer.upsert('execution', toExecutionStreamEntity(execution))`.\n5. `producer.upsert` calls `this.#instrumentation.startPublish(...)` synchronously, which calls `tracer.startSpan(...)`.\n\nIf the hook wraps step 4 with `context.with(extracted, () => producer.upsert(...))`, then the active context at step 5 is `extracted`, and the span inherits the trace id from `extracted` (which equals the stored traceparent). The `job.execute` span (started later at line 91) also uses the same stored traceparent as parent context, so it has the same trace id. **TC-14 passes.**\n\nNow let me check: is there a way the hook can fail to extract the traceparent? Look at `parseTraceparent` validation — it requires the traceparent to be a valid W3C traceparent string. If the stored traceparent is invalid (e.g., empty), `extractContext` returns `context.active()`. Let me check what `context.active()` is at the time of `create()` — at that point, no span is active, so it returns the root context. The span would get a **new trace id** (not the dispatch's). TC-14 would fail.\n\nBut wait — the `job.execute` span's parent context is from `getParentContextFromHeaders(traceHeaders)` where `traceHeaders` is the queue message's traceparent. So `job.execute`'s trace id equals the dispatch's. If `create()`'s publish span gets a new trace id (because the stored traceparent is missing), TC-14 fails for that record. But — the selector returns on the first match. If `create()` is the first match, TC-14 fails.\n\nSo D3 is **fragile** when the stored traceparent is absent. But in the normal case (the queue message always carries a traceparent), the stored traceparent will be valid and the join works.\n\nLet me also check whether D3 conflicts with D4 (no schema change). The plan claims D3 can be done without changing `WorkerExecutionZodSchema`. Since the stored `traceparent`/`tracestate` are already fields on `ExecutionRecord` (execution-state.ts:74,76), no schema change is needed. ✓\n\nNow let me verify the key claim about the published record's `correlationId` selector ambiguity. Looking at `select-flow-b-stream-change.ts:96-99`:\n\n```ts\nconst correlationId = streamChangeCorrelationId(change);\nif (correlationId) seen.add(correlationId);\nif (correlationId !== identity.correlationId) continue;\nassertFlowBChangeContext(identity, change);\nreturn { change, state, batchesRead, seenCorrelationIds: [...seen] };\n```\n\n**The selector returns the first change matching `correlationId === identity.correlationId`**. With D3, every record's `headers.traceparent.traceId === identity.traceId`, so TC-14 passes regardless of which one is returned. **The plan's claim holds.**\n\nNow let me check the **scope honesty** question — D1 says install on `startWorkerProcess` and `startCombinedProcess`. Look at `bin/runtime.ts:89-152`. The plan claims `startWorkerProcess` and `startCombinedProcess` both need the hook. Note that **the API service (`main.ts:67`) is the only place that currently installs the hook** — and it goes through `runtime.executionState.setMutationHook(createStreamMutationHook())`.\n\nFor D1, the plan says \"Installation must be idempotent and must not double-install when a process embeds the API service.\" Let me check `setMutationHook` (execution-state.ts:147-149):\n```ts\nsetMutationHook(hook: ExecutionMutationHook): void {\n  this.#onMutation = hook;\n}\n```\n\nYes — `setMutationHook` is **destructive**, not additive. It replaces any existing hook. So calling it twice with the same hook is fine, but if one process embeds the API service and another background process also calls it, the **last call wins**. This could be a problem if the API service already installed the hook and the background process also installs it (overwriting). But since both would install the same hook (`createStreamMutationHook()`), there's no functional difference. ✓\n\nNow let me check what `startCombinedProcess` does — does it use the same runtime? Looking at `bin/runtime.ts:127`: `const runtime = await createWorkersServiceRuntime();` — yes, it creates its own runtime instance. So no embedding.\n\nBut wait — could a generated project end up running both `startWorkerProcess` AND have `workers-api` running? In the typical Aspire resource graph, `workers-combined` and `workers-api` are separate resources (lines 44-65 of `workers-contribution.ts`).  /** Optional caller cancellation. */\n  readonly signal?: AbortSignal;\n}\n\n/** Stable failure categories translated by the durable-stream transport adapter. */\nexport type StreamProducerTransportFailureKindV1 =\n  | 'retryable'\n  | 'stale-epoch'\n  | 'sequence-gap'\n  | 'stream-closed'\n  | 'non-retryable'\n  | 'aborted';\n\n/** Transport failure understood by the application supervisor. */\nexport interface StreamProducerTransportFailureV1 {\n  /** Stable failure category. */\n  readonly kind: StreamProducerTransportFailureKindV1;\n  /** Operator-safe failure message. */\n  readonly message: string;\n  /** Current server epoch supplied with a stale-epoch response. */\n  readonly currentEpoch?: number;\n}\n","numLines":101,"startLine":30,"totalLines":130}}}

Recorded as `drift.md` D-2; re-checked before each eval dispatch.\n37\t\n38\t**Research.** #1405 researched in-session (small, fully specified) →\n39\t`slices/research-1405.md`, both defects confirmed at exact call sites with a line-cited call-site\n40\ttable for `#failActive`. #1398 research delegated to a Claude Opus sub-agent (read-only, `drift.md`\n41\tD-1 records the lane override) — report pending.\n42\t\n43\t## 2026-08-12 — #1398 research returned early (budget), root cause found\n44\t\n45\tThe delegated research sub-agent was **stopped on token budget** mid-pass and asked for concise\n46\tfindings rather than killed, so its evidence survived. Report: `slices/research-1398.md`.\n47\t\n48\tIt found the root cause and, importantly, found that **the repo already records it**: the two Flow-B\n49\tOTEL gates are deferred against #1398 with the reason \"workers-combined does not install the stream\n50\tmutation hook\" (`packages/cli/e2e/suites/scaffold/capability-suites.ts:23-34`). {"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"gen-1786521437-IY1xpeUqaF78MZ3cwKiJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","container":null,"content":[{"type":"text","text":"OK so `createStreamsInstrumentation` uses `getTracer('netscript.streams', version)` which returns `trace.getTracer(name, version)`. The `startSpan` is called via `this.#tracer.startSpan(...)`. If `this.#tracer` is the real OTel tracer, it accepts an optional 3rd Context arg. But the **StreamsTracerPort interface** only accepts `{ kind, attributes, links }`. So the wrapper doesn't pass a parent context.\n\nThat means the **only way** the publish span inherits the trace id is via `context.active()` at the time of the `startSpan` call. If the production hook wraps the `producer.upsert` call with `context.with(extractedContext, () => producer.upsert(...))`, then `context.active()` during `startSpan` returns the extracted context, and the new span uses it as parent.\n\nThis is **load-bearing** for D3. The plan's wording — \"publish under an OTel context extracted from the execution record's stored `traceparent`/`tracestate`\" — implies the wrapping. The implementation must do this. The plan **does not explicitly say \"wrap with `context.with(...)`\"**, but it's the only way to make D3 work.\n\nNow let me check whether the supervisors's traces are fed to the OTEL exporter with the proper span context. The Deno runtime's auto-registered OTel provider has a global `propagation` and `context`. When `context.with(...)` is used, the resulting context is active within the callback. Spans created inside that scope use the active context as parent. ✓\n\nNow let me check one more thing — whether the hook would fire from a microtask boundary that loses the context. The `setMutationHook` callback is called from `#save` (line 306) which is called from `validateExecution` (line 176) inside `create()`. The chain is synchronous within `await this.#kv.set(...)` because `setMutationHook` is called immediately after `set()` returns. The propagation is via JavaScript's microtask queue, so the active context within a single async function chain is preserved.\n\nBut wait — `await this.#kv.set(...)` suspends. After resumption, the active context is the **caller's context** (whatever was active when `create()` was called from `job-dispatcher.ts:73`). At that point, no `job.execute` span is active. So `context.active()` returns the **root** context (no parent). The traceparent stored in the record is the dispatch traceparent. If the hook wraps with `context.with(extractedFromStoredTraceparent, ...)`, then the publish span's trace id = stored traceparent's trace id = dispatch trace id = `job.execute` trace id. ✓\n\nNow let me verify the plan's claim about the **header trace id** vs **stored trace id**. The publish span's `spanContext().traceId` is the trace id of the publish span. Since the publish span's parent is the extracted context (which has the stored traceparent's trace id), the publish span's trace id = stored traceparent's trace id. The `formatTraceparent(span.spanContext())` (line 172) produces a `traceparent` header with this trace id. ✓\n\nNow let me check the plan's claim that **D5** removes the two OTEL gates from `SCAFFOLD_RUNTIME_DEFERRED_GATES`. The plan checks `suite-registry_test.ts:204-215` and removes them. Let me verify the test would change in the expected way:\n\nLooking at lines 204-215 of the test, the test `Deno.test('runtime suite omits the two #1398 OTEL gates ...')` checks that the two gates are NOT in the runtime suite. After D5, the gates should be IN the runtime suite. The test would need to flip `assertEquals(... false)` to `assertEquals(... true)`. The plan correctly names this as S2.\n\nBut the test at lines 209-221 (`runtime suites pin the exact #1398 OTEL deferral without widening it`) checks `SCAFFOLD_RUNTIME_DEFERRED_GATES` exactly. After D5 removes both gates from the deferral list, this test would need to change. The plan only mentions `suite-registry_test.ts:204-215`, but **lines 209-221 ALSO need to change**. GATE.RUNTIME_WAIT_GARNET), true);\n});\n\nDeno.test('sqlite runtime suite resolves its reduced-container defaults without mutating cache mode', () => {\n  const environmentVariable =  ];\n\n    // The fixture edits appsettings.json and regenerates; a declaration written\n    // after the AppHost is already up would prove nothing about the running\n    // resource, and the assertEquals(sqliteGateIds.includes(GATE.BEHAVIOR_LIVE_DB_ENDPOINT), false);\n});\n\nDeno.test('runtime suite selects mssql database resource wait for mssql', () => {\n  const runtime =
